People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi Dr. Smith. Thank you so much for seeing me today. 

Um... well, I guess I'm here because I've been doing some... slightly unusual things lately? At work—I'm a bookkeeping clerk—and at school. It's a little embarrassing to talk about, honestly, because I know it probably sounds strange to other people. But I've just started doing things that don't really make sense. 

Like, instead of filing, I've been lining receipts all the way across the floor. Or I'll catch myself tapping my calculator in these strict sets of nine. And... well, sometimes I just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, just waiting until someone walks by and asks if I'm okay. 

It's probably just a little stress-related habit, right? It sounds a lot worse out loud than it actually is, I promise. I just wanted to check if this is the right place to talk about it... am I doing the right thing by coming in?

Um... well, it's... I know it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud. It's just that sometimes, when I'm looking at the spreadsheets... certain invoice totals, or account numbers, or even just the due dates... I get this really strong feeling that they're messages. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I hope it's okay to say this, because I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or anything... but yeah, the voice and the numbers... they're pretty much there all the time. 

It's actually been going on for about eight months now. The voice talking to me and that feeling that the numbers are trying to send me messages... they happen almost every day. I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right and giving you the kind of information you need... did I answer that okay?\n\nDoctor: Aside from these movements, have you noticed anything else unusual lately—like hearing voices or sounds other people don't hear, seeing things others don't see, or having thoughts or beliefs that others have told you seem unusual or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess maybe there is one other little thing? It's really nothing major, I promise. It's just... \n\nSometimes, like when I'm all alone at my desk, or just walking home from my classes, I... I'll hear a voice. Just a man's voice, really quick. He'll say something like, um, 'check line seven,' or 'you missed the sign.' And it sounds so clear that I actually look around, even though I know nobody's there. \n\nBut it's probably just my brain being tired, right? Like an echo from work or something? I know it sounds a little weird, but it doesn't happen all the time. I'm not, like, going crazy or anything. \n\nIs that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these behaviors and hearing that voice—has it been going on for weeks, or longer, like several months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it's been... maybe a little longer than just a few weeks. If I really think about it, it's probably been about eight months now? For the voice, I mean. And, um... I guess also for this... this feeling I get sometimes that the numbers on my spreadsheets are... like little messages? \n\nI'm sorry I didn't mention the number thing a second ago, I just... I didn't want you to think I sounded too weird. But yeah, it's been about eight months for both of those things. It really hasn't been a problem, though, I promise. I just work right through it. \n\nIs that... does that give you the timeline you needed? I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: When you're standing at the copier with your hands raised, how long do you typically stay like that, and if someone tries to move your arms or reposition you, do you resist, stay stiff, or let them move you freely?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I don't really stand there for very long at all. Just a few seconds, really. It's just a quick stretch, almost. \n\nAnd, um, nobody's ever actually tried to move my arms or anything. I think if they did, I'd just put them down right away. I definitely wouldn't fight them or stay stiff or anything weird like that. I'd probably just apologize and get out of their way. \n\nIt's really just a little habit, nothing disruptive. I hope that answers your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: When you notice the numbers seeming like messages, can you tell me more about what you believe they mean—do you think they're specifically directed at you, perhaps warning you or trying to tell you something important, and how convinced are you that this is really happening?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... I know it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud. It's just that sometimes, when I'm looking at the spreadsheets... certain invoice totals, or account numbers, or even just the due dates... I get this really strong feeling that they're messages. Like they're specifically about me. \n\nAnd, um, I know my supervisor—he's really nice—he's sat down and shown me that they're just normal entries, just regular accounting data. And I agree with him when he shows me, I really do! But... even then, I still get this lingering feeling that they're somehow tracking me, or testing to see if I understand the pattern. \n\nI'm not like, completely obsessed with it or anything, I promise. It's just a feeling I get sometimes. I still get all my work done on time. I hope that doesn't make me sound too... out there? I'm trying to be as honest as I can so you have what you need. Is that the kind of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Beyond feeling like the numbers are testing or tracking you, do you ever feel that someone or something is trying to harm you, control your actions, or is out to get you in some way?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, definitely not. I've never felt like anyone was trying to hurt me or... or control me, or anything scary like that. \n\nI mean, everyone at the office is really nice, and I get along with all of them. I've never felt like anyone was out to get me. It's really just that weird feeling with the numbers sometimes, but I definitely don't feel unsafe or like I'm in danger. \n\nI really try to keep to myself and just do a good job, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I'm paranoid or anything like that. Does that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering these right for you.\n\nDoctor: Over these past several months, have you noticed any changes in your emotions or motivation—like feeling less interested in things you used to enjoy, having a flatter or less expressive mood, or wanting to withdraw from friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm totally withdrawn or anything like that. I still care about people, I really do. It's just... I guess lately, I haven't been as energetic about socializing. \n\nLike, I might let a text sit unanswered for a bit, or I'll skip hanging out after work or class. And sometimes, even when my friends are talking about things we used to get really excited about together—like going to music shows or working on art projects—I just... I kind of feel a bit flat, I guess? Like the excitement isn't quite there the way it used to be. \n\nBut I'm still friendly with everyone, and I don't want them to think I'm ignoring them on purpose. I just feel a little less motivated to go out right now. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad friend or anything? I just wanted to be honest about how I've been feeling lately. Does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed times when your thoughts feel jumbled or hard to organize, or when others have told you that your speech is difficult to follow or jumps between unrelated topics?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I really try my best to be clear when I talk to people. I definitely don't want you to think I can't communicate or do my job! But... I guess, sometimes, if I'm trying to explain something a bit complicated at work—like if I'm showing someone why a ledger is off—my mind just moves a little fast? \n\nLike, I'll start explaining the math, but then my thoughts will sort of just jump to my tuition deadlines, and then maybe some tax rules, and then... I don't know, studio lighting, or those number patterns I mentioned earlier. They all feel connected to me in the moment, but, um, people at the office have gently mentioned that they can't quite follow how I got from one point to the next. \n\nI always apologize when that happens, and I really try to slow down and start over for them. I don't mean to be confusing, I just have a lot of connections going on in my head at once sometimes. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is? I'm really trying to give you the right information... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Over these past eight months, have you noticed your performance at work or school declining, or having more trouble managing daily responsibilities like chores or self-care, compared to before these experiences started?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard, I promise. I don't want to sound like I'm slacking off or anything, because I really do care about doing a good job. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have been a little bit harder lately? Like, at school, I actually... I ended up failing one of my important course modules. I just couldn't seem to keep up the way I usually do. And at work, my ledgers... well, they started coming back with some, um, serious errors. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit, but I've even had to ask for help with tasks that used to be super routine for me. I just can't seem to reliably finish them on my own right now. I really hope my boss isn't too upset with me. I'm trying my best to get back on track, I really am. I hope it's okay that I shared that with you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details.\n\nDoctor: Of everything you've described—the voice, the feeling that numbers are tracking or testing you, the trouble organizing your thoughts, and the hand movements—which of these feels like it bothers or preoccupies you the most right now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, they all kind of bother me a little bit, but... The voice talking to me and that feeling that the numbers are trying to send me messages... they happen almost every day. I really try my best to ignore them and just focus on my work, but they're definitely the most prominent thing on my mind, even more than the tapping or the receipts. \n\nI know eight months sounds like a long time to let this go on without saying anything. Oh, thank you for explaining. I really want to give you the most helpful answer, so I appreciate you bearing with me. 

Um... I guess it's kind of a mix, but maybe more the trouble organizing my thoughts and just not having that drive? I'm really embarrassed to admit this, because I pride myself on doing a good job, but... lately, my ledgers have started coming back with some serious errors. I feel terrible making extra work for my supervisor, she's so nice to me, but I've actually had to ask for help because I just can't reliably finish the work that used to be completely routine for me. 

And with school... I, um, I actually failed an important course module recently. I really don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or giving up, I promise I'm putting in the effort! It's just getting really hard to keep all my thoughts straight to get the work done. 

Does that make sense? I hope I answered that right for you.
